# 2022 FIFA World Cup

"2022 World Cup" redirects here. For other competitions of that name, see [2022 World Cup (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_World_Cup_\(disambiguation\)).

"FIFA 2022" redirects here. For the video game, see [*FIFA 22*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_22).

The **2022 FIFA World Cup** was the 22nd [FIFA World Cup](11370.md), the quadrennial world championship for national [football](10568.md) teams organised by [FIFA](11049.md). It took place in Qatar from 20 November to 18 December 2022, after the country was awarded the hosting rights in 2010. It was the first World Cup to be held in the [Middle East](19323.md) and the [Arabian Peninsula](47858.md), and the second in an Asian country after the [2002 tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_FIFA_World_Cup) in South Korea and Japan.[^cite_note-1]

This tournament was the last with 32 participating teams, before increasing to 48 for the [2026 World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_World_Cup). To avoid the extremes of [Qatar's hot climate in summers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Qatar#Climate),[^cite_note-auto5-2] the event was held in November and December, becoming the first to take place in the end of the year and outside the traditional months of May, June, and July.[^cite_note-auto5-2][^cite_note-3] It was held over a reduced time frame of 29 days with 64 matches played in [eight venues](#Stadiums) across [five cities](#Cities). [Qatar](1111350.md) entered the event—their first World Cup—automatically as the host's national team, alongside 31 teams determined by the [qualification process](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification).

[Morocco](1024865.md) made history by becoming the first African nation to reach the semi-final of a World Cup, falling short 0–2 against [France](250197.md).[^cite_note-4] [Argentina](454699.md) were crowned the champions after winning [the final](70432805.md) against the [title holder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_FIFA_World_Cup_final) [France](250197.md) 4–2 on penalties following a 3–3 draw after extra time. It was Argentina's third title and their first since [1986](157231.md). Argentina were the first nation from outside of Europe to win the tournament since [2002](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_FIFA_World_Cup). French player [Kylian Mbappé](48711857.md) became the first player to score a [hat-trick](411541.md) in a World Cup final since [Geoff Hurst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geoff_Hurst) in the [1966 final](61629.md) and won the [Golden Boot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_Golden_Boot) as he scored the most goals (eight) during the tournament. Mbappé also became the first player to score in two consecutive finals since [Vavá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vav%C3%A1) of Brazil did the same in 1958 and 1962.

Argentine captain [Lionel Messi](2150841.md) was voted the tournament's best player, winning his second [Golden Ball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_Golden_Ball). The tournament has been considered exceptionally poetic as the capstone of his career, with the win fulfilling for some commentators a previously unmet criterion to be regarded as the greatest player of all time.[^cite_note-NYT2022-5] Teammates [Emiliano Martínez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emiliano_Mart%C3%ADnez) and [Enzo Fernández](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enzo_Fern%C3%A1ndez) won the [Golden Glove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_Golden_Glove), awarded to the tournament's best [goalkeeper](5348091.md), and the [Young Player Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_Young_Player_Award), awarded to the tournament's best young player, respectively. With 172 goals, the tournament set a record for the highest number of goals scored in the 32-team format, with every participating team scoring at least one goal.

The choice to host the World Cup in Qatar [attracted significant criticism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_controversies), with concerns raised over the country's [treatment of migrant workers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_issues_involving_the_2022_FIFA_World_Cup), [women](#LGBT_and_women's_rights), and [members of the LGBTQ+ community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Qatar), as well as Qatar's climate and [lack of a strong football culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_controversies#Qatari_football_record). There were also allegations that Qatar used bribery to obtain hosting rights, as well as allegations of [wider FIFA corruption](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_FIFA_corruption_case).[^cite_note-14]

## Schedule

Unlike previous FIFA World Cups, which are typically played in June and July, because of [Qatar's intense summer heat and often fairly high humidity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Qatar#Climate),[^cite_note-auto5-2][^cite_note-auto6-15][^cite_note-16] the 2022 World Cup was played in November and December.[^cite_note-Atlantic_2022-6][^cite_note-auto5-2] As a result, the World Cup was unusually staged in the middle of the seasons of many domestic association football leagues, which started in late July or August, including all of the major European leagues, which had been obliged to incorporate extended breaks into their domestic schedules to accommodate the World Cup. Major European competitions had scheduled their respective competitions group matches to be played before the World Cup, to avoid playing group matches the following year.[^cite_note-17]

The match schedule was confirmed by FIFA on 15 July 2020.[^cite_note-schedule_confirmed-18] The group stage was set to begin on 21 November, with four matches every day. Later, the schedule was tweaked by moving the Qatar vs Ecuador game to 20 November, after Qatar lobbied FIFA to allow their team to open the tournament.[^cite_note-fifa_2022-08-11-19][^cite_note-20][^cite_note-21] The final was played on 18 December 2022, [National Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Day_\(Qatar\)), at [Lusail Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lusail_Stadium).[^cite_note-schedule_confirmed-18][^cite_note-schedule-22] Unlike previous tournaments where the match venues and kick-off times for each fixture were set prior to the draw, the assignment of group fixtures for each matchday to a specific venue and kick-off time was only made after the final draw, with the teams of each specific fixture known. This was due to the close proximity of the venues, which allowed the organisers to optimise stadium allocation for spectators and kick-off times for television audiences.[^cite_note-schedule_confirmed-18]

The matches for each group were allocated to the following stadiums:[^cite_note-schedule-22]

- Groups A, B, E, F: [Al Bayt Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Bayt_Stadium), [Khalifa International Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khalifa_International_Stadium), [Al Thumama Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Thumama_Stadium), [Ahmad bin Ali Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmad_bin_Ali_Stadium)

- Groups C, D, G, H: [Lusail Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lusail_Stadium), [Stadium 974](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stadium_974), [Education City Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_City_Stadium), [Al Janoub Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Janoub_Stadium)

FIFA confirmed the group stage venue and kick-off times on 1 April 2022, following the draw.[^cite_note-23][^cite_note-24]

## Prize money

In April 2022, FIFA announced the prizes for all participating nations. Each qualified team received \$1.5 million before the competition to cover preparation costs with each team receiving at least \$9 million in prize money. This edition's total prize pool was \$440 million, \$40 million greater than the prize pool of the [previous tournament](13327177.md).[^cite_note-25]

| Place | Teams | Amount (in millions) |  |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Per team | Total |  |  |
| Champions | 1 | \$42 | \$42 |
| Runner-up | 1 | \$30 | \$30 |
| Third place | 1 | \$27 | \$27 |
| Fourth place | 1 | \$25 | \$25 |
| 5th–8th place (quarter-finals) | 4 | \$17 | \$68 |
| 9th–16th place (round of 16) | 8 | \$13 | \$104 |
| 17th–32nd place (group stage) | 16 | \$9 | \$144 |
| Total | 32 | \$440 |  |

## Rule changes

The tournament featured new [substitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Substitute_\(association_football\)) rules whereby teams could make up to five substitutions in normal time, and an additional substitution in [extra time](633242.md#Association_football).[^cite_note-26][^cite_note-27][^cite_note-28] In addition, it was the first World Cup to feature [concussion substitutions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Substitute_\(association_football\)#Concussion_substitute), whereby each team was permitted to use a maximum of one concussion substitute during a match. A concussion substitution did not count towards a team's quota of regular substitutions.[^cite_note-29] Iranian goalkeeper [Alireza Beiranvand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alireza_Beiranvand) suffered a concussion in [his country's opening match against England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_Group_B#England_vs_Iran) and was replaced by [Hossein Hosseini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hossein_Hosseini_\(footballer,_born_1992\)). This was the first use of a dedicated concussion substitute during a World Cup.[^cite_note-30]

## Host selection

Main articles: [2018 and 2022 FIFA World Cup bids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_and_2022_FIFA_World_Cup_bids) and [Qatar 2022 FIFA World Cup bid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qatar_2022_FIFA_World_Cup_bid)



Russian President [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) handing over the symbolic relay baton for the hosting rights of the 2022 FIFA World Cup to Qatar's Emir [Tamim bin Hamad Al Thani](6146151.md) in June 2018.

The [bidding procedure to host the 2018 and 2022 FIFA World Cups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_and_2022_FIFA_World_Cup_bids) began in January 2009. National associations had until 2 February 2009 to register interest.[^cite_note-31] Initially, 11 bids were made for the [2018 FIFA World Cup](13327177.md), but Mexico withdrew from proceedings,[^cite_note-32][^cite_note-33][^cite_note-34] and [Indonesia's bid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesia_2022_FIFA_World_Cup_bid) was rejected by FIFA in February 2010 after the Indonesian Football Association failed to submit a letter of [Indonesian government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Indonesia) guarantee to support the bid.[^cite_note-reject-35]

After [UEFA](32332.md) were guaranteed to host the 2018 event, members of UEFA were no longer in contention to host in 2022.[^cite_note-36] There were five bids remaining for the 2022 FIFA World Cup: Australia, Japan, Qatar, South Korea, and the United States. The 22-member [FIFA Executive Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_Executive_Committee) convened in [Zürich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Z%C3%BCrich), Switzerland, on 2 December 2010 to vote to select the hosts of both tournaments.[^cite_note-2018+2022_bids-37][^cite_note-38] Two FIFA executive committee members were suspended before the vote in relation to allegations of corruption regarding their votes.[^cite_note-39] The decision to host the 2022 World Cup in Qatar, which was graded as having "high operational risk",[^cite_note-40] generated criticism from media commentators.[^cite_note-guardian-41] It was criticised by many as being part of the FIFA corruption scandals,[^cite_note-42] which led to the [2015 FIFA corruption case](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_FIFA_corruption_case).

The voting patterns were as follows:[^cite_note-43]

| Bidders | Votes |  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Round 1 | Round 2 | Round 3 | Round 4 |  |
| **Qatar** | 11 | 10 | 11 | 14 |
| United States | 3 | 5 | 6 | 8 |
| South Korea | 4 | 5 | 5 | Eliminated |
| Japan | 3 | 2 | Eliminated |  |
| Australia | 1 | Eliminated |  |  |

### Cost of hosting the tournament

At an estimated cost of over \$220 billion,[^cite_note-44] it is the most expensive World Cup ever held to date; this figure is disputed by Qatari officials, including organising [CEO](52234.md) Nasser Al Khater, who said the true cost was \$8 billion, and other figures related to overall infrastructure development since the World Cup was awarded to Qatar in 2010.[^cite_note-45]

## Venues

Further information: [List of football stadiums in Qatar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_football_stadiums_in_Qatar)



Seven of the eight venues, including [Lusail Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lusail_Stadium), the venue for the final, were new stadiums built specifically for the 2022 FIFA World Cup. The eighth was heavily renovated.

The first five proposed venues for the World Cup were unveiled at the beginning of March 2010. Qatar intended that the stadiums should reflect its history and culture, and for the designs to meet the following terms of reference: legacy, comfort, accessibility, and sustainability.[^cite_note-sc.qa_stadiums-46] The stadiums were equipped with cooling systems that aim to reduce temperatures within the stadium by up to 20 °C (36 °F).[^cite_note-auto32-47][^cite_note-auto8-48]

Their marketing included statements describing the stadiums as [zero waste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zero_waste), and the upper tiers of the stadiums will be disassembled after the World Cup and donated to countries with less developed sports infrastructure.[^cite_note-auto32-47][^cite_note-auto8-48] Qatar aspired to be compliant and certified by the [Global Sustainability Assessment System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Sustainability_Assessment_System) (GSAS) for all the World Cup stadiums. All of the five stadium projects launched were designed by German architect [Albert Speer & Partners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albert_Speer_Jr.).[^cite_note-49] The Al Bayt and Al Wakrah stadiums were the only indoor stadiums of the eight used.[^cite_note-50]



Some venues, such as the 68,000-seat [Al Bayt Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Bayt_Stadium), were planned to have their upper tiers of seating removed to reduce capacity after the tournament, however this is yet to happen.

In an April 2013 report by [Merrill Lynch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merrill_Lynch), the organisers in Qatar requested that FIFA approve a smaller number of stadiums due to the growing costs.[^cite_note-51] [Bloomberg](3057255.md) said that Qatar wished to cut the number of venues to eight or nine from the twelve originally planned.[^cite_note-52] By April 2017, FIFA had yet to finalise the number of stadiums Qatar must have readied in five years' time. Qatar's Supreme Committee for Delivery and Legacy (SC) said it expected there would be eight in and near [Doha](26214389.md), with the exception of Al Khor.[^cite_note-53][^cite_note-sc.qa_stadiums-46]

Eight stadiums in five Qatari cities were built or renovated for the FIFA World Cup. Between 2010 (when Qatar were announced as hosts) and 2022, seven of the eight stadiums were built (some in place of older, outdated venues) and the [Khalifa International Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khalifa_International_Stadium) was renovated for the tournament.

- Al Khor: Al Bayt Stadium (new). The first piles were driven into the ground in 2014. On 30 November 2021 it hosted its first match.

- Lusail: Lusail Stadium (new). The construction of the stadium was started on 11 April 2017. It was completed on 21 November 2021.

- Al Rayyan: Ahmad bin Ali Stadium (new). The stadium was built on the demolished [former stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmed_bin_Ali_Stadium_\(original\)) site. It was commissioned on 18 December 2020.

- Al Rayyan: Education City Stadium (new). It was completed on 15 June 2020.

- Al Rayyan: Khalifa International Stadium (heavily renovated). The largest stadium in the country at the time, it was closed for renovation in 2014. It was commissioned in May 2017.

- Doha: Al Thumama Stadium (new). Construction was completed on 21 October 2021.

- Doha: Stadium 974 (new). Construction commenced in 2018. It was completed on 30 November 2021 and had hosted some of the matches in 2021 FIFA Arab Cup.

- Al Wakrah: Al Janoub Stadium (new). Construction officially started in 2014 and was completed on 16 May 2019.

Of these eight stadiums, all but two have since been either dismantled, or have been moderately or heavily modified. The [Stadium 974](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stadium_974) was supposed to be dismantled after the tournament; as of November 2024 however, this has not happened and the stadium has sat abandoned in its original site—this stadium was the first planned temporary stadium ever used for a FIFA World Cup.[^cite_note-54] [Lusail Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lusail_Stadium), [Al Bayt Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Bayt_Stadium), [Ahmad bin Ali Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmad_bin_Ali_Stadium), [Education City Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_City_Stadium), [Al Thumama Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Thumama_Stadium) and [Al Janoub Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Janoub_Stadium) will be moderately or heavily modified so as to reduce their capacity by half.[^cite_note-55] The [Khalifa International Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khalifa_International_Stadium) (latter along with the [Stadium 974](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stadium_974)) are the only largely unmodified stadium that were used for this tournament.

### Team base camps

Each participating team established a "base camp", where they stayed and trained before and during the tournament.[^cite_note-72][^cite_note-73] This World Cup was the most compact since the inaugural edition [in 1930](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1930_FIFA_World_Cup), with 24 of the 32 teams being within a 10 km radius of each other, and concentrated in the [Doha Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doha_Metropolitan_Area). It was the first World Cup since 1930 in which players did not need to take flights to matches, and could remain at the same base camp during the entire tournament.[^cite_note-campsa2-74][^cite_note-campsb2-75]

### Security

The Qatari government employed about 50,000 security personnel, including police departments and military forces. These personnel came from at least thirteen countries, including Poland, Germany, France, Kuwait, Jordan, Italy, Palestine, Spain, Pakistan (4,500 Army troops), Turkey (3,000 riot police personnel), the US, Saudi Arabia and the United Kingdom.[^cite_note-EA2-76][^cite_note-\:9b2-77][^cite_note-78]

## Teams

### Qualification

Main article: [2022 FIFA World Cup qualification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification)

FIFA's [six continental confederations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_confederations) organised their own [qualifying competitions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_qualification). All 211 [FIFA member associations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_FIFA_member_associations) were eligible to enter qualification. The [Qatari national team](1111350.md), as hosts, qualified automatically for the tournament. They still participated in the Asian qualifying stage as the first two rounds also acted as [qualification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_AFC_Asian_Cup_qualification) for the [2023 AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_AFC_Asian_Cup).[^cite_note-79] Since Qatar reached the final stage as winners in their group, [Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanon_national_football_team), the fifth-best second place team, advanced instead.[^cite_note-80] Qatar were the only team making their debut in the FIFA World Cup, becoming the first hosts to make their tournament debut since [Italy](362466.md) in [1934](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1934_FIFA_World_Cup).

Of the 32 nations qualified to play at the 2022 FIFA World Cup, 24 countries competed at the previous tournament in 2018,[^cite_note-81] including [France](250197.md), the reigning [World Cup champions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_FIFA_World_Cup_finals),[^cite_note-82] who qualified for the event through European qualification rather than automatically as champions.

#### Debutant and returnees

- Qatar were the only team making their debut in the FIFA World Cup, becoming the first hosts to make their tournament debut since [Italy](362466.md) in [1934](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1934_FIFA_World_Cup).

- [Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wales_national_football_team) made their first appearance in 64 years, the longest-ever gap for any team; their only previous participation having been in [1958](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1958_FIFA_World_Cup).

  [^cite_note-\:7-83]

- [Canada](723688.md) returned after 36 years, their only prior appearance being in [1986](157231.md).

  [^cite_note-84]

- The [Netherlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netherlands_national_football_team), [Ecuador](1016797.md), [Ghana](1029620.md), [Cameroon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cameroon_national_football_team), and the [United States](582488.md) returned to the tournament after missing the 2018 edition.

- [Ghana](1029620.md) were the lowest ranked team to qualify, ranked 61st.

  [^cite_note-85]

As of [2026](36581929.md), this was the last time [Cameroon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cameroon_national_football_team), [Costa Rica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Costa_Rica_national_football_team), [Denmark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denmark_national_football_team), [Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poland_national_football_team), [Serbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbia_national_football_team), and [Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wales_national_football_team) qualified for the World Cup, and the last time [Algeria](1029528.md), [Austria](695226.md), [Bosnia and Herzegovina](1008974.md), [Cape Verde](1205575.md), [Colombia](680175.md), [Curaçao](24862626.md), the [Czech Republic](729739.md), [DR Congo](1030243.md), [Egypt](1022953.md), [Haiti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haiti_national_football_team), [Iraq](1039652.md), [Ivory Coast](1039856.md), [Jordan](1090065.md), [New Zealand](1023035.md), [Norway](203277.md), [Panama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panama_national_football_team), [Paraguay](804025.md), [Scotland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scotland_national_football_team), [South Africa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa_national_football_team), [Sweden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sweden_men's_national_football_team), [Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkey_men's_national_football_team) and [Uzbekistan](1032413.md) failed to qualify.

### Squads

Main article: [2022 FIFA World Cup squads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_squads)

Before submitting their final squad for the tournament, teams named a provisional squad of up to 55 players. Teams were required to have their 55-player roster submitted to FIFA by 21 October.[^cite_note-87] Teams were required to name their final squads by 13 November.[^cite_note-88] In August 2022, FIFA increased the final squad size to 26 players from a total of 23 players at the [2018 World Cup](13327177.md).[^cite_note-89] All teams had a total of 26 players in their final squads except for [France](250197.md) and [Iran](272865.md), who chose 25 players.[^cite_note-90][^cite_note-91]

### Draw

Main article: [2022 FIFA World Cup seeding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_seeding)

The final draw was held at the Doha Exhibition and Convention Center in [Doha](26214389.md), Qatar,[^cite_note-92] on 1 April 2022,[^cite_note-93] 19\:00 [AST](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabia_Standard_Time), prior to the completion of [qualification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification). The two winners of the [inter-confederation play-offs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_\(inter-confederation_play-offs\)) and the winner of the [Path A of the UEFA play-offs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_UEFA_second_round#Path_A) were not known at the time of the draw.[^cite_note-94] The draw was attended by 2,000 guests and was led by [Carli Lloyd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carli_Lloyd), [Jermaine Jenas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jermaine_Jenas) and sports broadcaster Samantha Johnson, assisted by the likes of [Cafu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cafu) (Brazil), [Lothar Matthäus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lothar_Matth%C3%A4us) (Germany), [Adel Ahmed Malalla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adel_Ahmed_Malalla) (Qatar), [Ali Daei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Daei) (Iran), [Bora Milutinović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bora_Milutinovi%C4%87) (Serbia/Mexico), [Jay-Jay Okocha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jay-Jay_Okocha) (Nigeria), [Rabah Madjer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rabah_Madjer) (Algeria), and [Tim Cahill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tim_Cahill) (Australia).[^cite_note-drawProcedure-95][^cite_note-96]

For the draw, 32 teams were allocated into four pots based on the [FIFA Men's World Rankings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_Men's_World_Rankings) of 31 March 2022.[^cite_note-97] Pot 1 contained host Qatar (who were automatically assigned to position A1) and the best seven teams. Pot 2 contained the next best eight teams, with the next best eight teams into pot 3. Pot 4 contained the five lowest-ranked teams, along with the placeholders for the two inter-confederation play-off winners and the UEFA Path A play-off winner. Teams from the same confederation could not be drawn into the same group except for UEFA teams, for which there was at least one and no more than two per group.[^cite_note-auto2-98]

This principle also applied to the placeholder teams, with constraints applying based on the confederation of both potential winners of each play-off tie. The draw started with pot 1 and ended with pot 4, with each team selected then allocated into the first available group alphabetically. The position for the team within the group would then be drawn (for the purpose of the [match schedule](#Schedule)), with the pot 1 teams automatically drawn into position 1 of each group.[^cite_note-auto2-98] The pots for the draw are shown below.[^cite_note-drawProcedure-95]

| [Qatar](1111350.md) (51) **(H)**   [Brazil](149286.md) (1)   [Belgium](679716.md) (2)   [France](250197.md) (3)   [Argentina](454699.md) (4)   [England](9904.md) (5)   [Spain](609825.md) (7)   [Portugal](576328.md) (8) | [Mexico](808402.md) (9)   [Netherlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netherlands_national_football_team) (10)   [Denmark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denmark_national_football_team) (11)   [Germany](250204.md) (12)   [Uruguay](679782.md) (13)    [Switzerland](681862.md) (14)   [United States](582488.md) (15)   [Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatia_national_football_team) (16) | [Senegal](1017436.md) (20)   [Iran](272865.md) (21)   [Japan](993546.md) (23)   [Morocco](1024865.md) (24)   [Serbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serbia_national_football_team) (25)   [Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poland_national_football_team) (26)   [South Korea](1018627.md) (29)   [Tunisia](1029953.md) (35) | [Cameroon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cameroon_national_football_team) (37)   [Canada](723688.md) (38)   [Ecuador](1016797.md) (46)   [Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi_Arabia_national_football_team) (49)   [Ghana](1029620.md) (60)   [UEFA Path A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_UEFA_second_round#Path_A) winners[^cite_note-placeholder-99]   [CONCACAF–OFC play-off](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_\(CONCACAF%E2%80%93OFC_play-off\)) winners[^cite_note-placeholder-99]   [AFC–CONMEBOL play-off](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_\(AFC%E2%80%93CONMEBOL_play-off\)) winners[^cite_note-placeholder-99] |

#### Draw result

The eight groups were formed randomly, selecting one team from each of the four pots. Two teams from the same confederation could not be placed into the same group, with the exception of UEFA teams, where up to two teams could be in the same group. The only team whose position in the draw was predetermined was the host Qatar, who were placed into position A1.

## Officiating

Main article: [2022 FIFA World Cup officials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_officials)

In May 2022, FIFA announced the list of 36 referees, 69 assistant referees, and 24 [video assistant referees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Video_assistant_referee) for the tournament. Of the 36 referees, FIFA included two each from Argentina, Brazil, England, and France.[^cite_note-103][^cite_note-104]

For the first time women referees officiated games at a major men's tournament.[^cite_note-105] France's [Stéphanie Frappart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St%C3%A9phanie_Frappart), [Salima Mukansanga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salima_Mukansanga) from Rwanda, and [Yoshimi Yamashita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoshimi_Yamashita) from Japan became the first female referees to be appointed to a men's World Cup.[^cite_note-106] Frappart previously oversaw the [2019 FIFA Women's World Cup final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_FIFA_Women's_World_Cup_final).[^cite_note-107] They were joined by three female assistant referees, Neuza Back, [Kathryn Nesbitt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kathryn_Nesbitt), and Karen Díaz Medina. Frappart then officially became the first-ever female referee to officiate a World Cup match when she worked the [Costa Rica vs Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_Group_E#Costa_Rica_vs_Germany) match in [Group E](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_Group_E) on 1 December.[^cite_note-108]

Gambian referee [Bakary Gassama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bakary_Gassama) and Argentine assistant referee [Juan Pablo Belatti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Pablo_Belatti) were among the officials to serve at their third World Cup. Belatti was an assistant referee in the [2018 final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_FIFA_World_Cup_final).[^cite_note-109][^cite_note-110][^cite_note-111] Other returning officials included referees [César Arturo Ramos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%C3%A9sar_Arturo_Ramos) of Mexico and [Janny Sikazwe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Janny_Sikazwe) of Zambia, and Iranian assistant referee [Mohammadreza Mansouri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammadreza_Mansouri).[^cite_note-112][^cite_note-113][^cite_note-114]

On 15 December 2022, FIFA announced that [Polish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Football_Association) referee [Szymon Marciniak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Szymon_Marciniak) would adjudicate the final.[^cite_note-115]

## Opening ceremony

Main article: [2022 FIFA World Cup opening ceremony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_opening_ceremony)

The [opening ceremony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_opening_ceremony) took place on Sunday, 20 November 2022 at the Al Bayt Stadium in Al Khor, prior to the [opening match of the tournament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_Group_A#Qatar_vs_Ecuador) between hosts Qatar and Ecuador.[^cite_note-fifa_2022-08-11-19] It included appearances by [Morgan Freeman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morgan_Freeman) and [Ghanim Al-Muftah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghanim_Al-Muftah), along with performances by South Korean singer and [BTS](39862325.md) member [Jungkook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jungkook) and Qatari singer [Fahad Al Kubaisi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fahad_Al_Kubaisi).[^cite_note-116][^cite_note-117] It was the first time that the [Qur'an](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quran) had been recited as part of the opening ceremony.[^cite_note-118] During the ceremony, [Emir](399230.md) [Tamim bin Hamad Al Thani](6146151.md) declared the tournament open.[^cite_note-119]

## Group stage



Result of countries participating in the 2022 FIFA World Cup

All times are local, [AST](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabia_Standard_Time) ([UTC+3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UTC+03\:00)).

The group stage was played from 20 November to 2 December.[^cite_note-120] Competing countries were divided into eight groups of four teams (groups A to H). Teams in each group played one another in a [round-robin](939742.md), where the top two teams advanced to the [knockout stage](#Knockout_stage).

| Tie-breaking criteria for group play |
| --- |
| The ranking of teams in the group stage was determined as follows:[^cite_note-regulations-121] Points obtained in all group matches; Goal difference in all group matches; Number of goals scored in all group matches; Points obtained in the matches played between the teams in question; Goal difference in the matches played between the teams in question; Number of goals scored in the matches played between the teams in question; Fair play points in all group matches (only one deduction could be applied to a player in a single match): Yellow card: −1 points;Indirect red card (second yellow card): −3 points;Direct red card: −4 points;Yellow card and direct red card: −5 points; Drawing of lots. |
|  |

### Group A

Main article: [2022 FIFA World Cup Group A](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_Group_A)

The first match of the tournament was held between Qatar and Ecuador in Group A. Ecuador had a disallowed goal in the opening minutes,[^cite_note-122] but eventually won 2–0 with two goals from [Enner Valencia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enner_Valencia).[^cite_note-123] Qatar became the first host nation to lose their opening match at a World Cup.[^cite_note-124][^cite_note-125][^cite_note-126] Many Qatar natives were seen leaving the game before the end, with [ESPN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESPN) reporting that two-thirds of the attendance had left.[^cite_note-127][^cite_note-128] The other starting match in group A was won by the Netherlands 2–0 over Senegal. [Cody Gakpo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cody_Gakpo) scored the opening goal in the 84th minute and [Davy Klaassen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davy_Klaassen) added a second in stoppage time.[^cite_note-129] Senegal faced Qatar in the third match of the group; [Boulaye Dia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boulaye_Dia) capitalised on a slip by [Boualem Khoukhi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boualem_Khoukhi) to put Senegal 1–0 ahead. [Famara Diédhiou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Famara_Di%C3%A9dhiou) scored a second with a header, before [Mohammed Muntari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_Muntari) scored Qatar's first-ever goal at a World Cup to reduce the deficit back to one. Senegal eventually won the match 3–1 after an 84th-minute goal by [Bamba Dieng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bamba_Dieng). With this result, Qatar became the first team to be eliminated from the tournament, as well as becoming the first host nation to ever be knocked out of the tournament after two games.[^cite_note-130] Gakpo scored his second goal of the tournament as the Netherlands led Ecuador; however, Valencia scored an equaliser in the 49th minute.[^cite_note-131] The Netherlands won 2–0 against Qatar following goals by Gakpo and [Frenkie de Jong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frenkie_de_Jong) to win the group, while Qatar attained the distinction of being the first home nation to lose all three group matches.[^cite_note-132] Senegal faced Ecuador to determine the second knockout round qualifier. At the end of the first half, [Ismaïla Sarr](51591292.md) scored a penalty kick to put Senegal ahead. In the 67th minute, [Moisés Caicedo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mois%C3%A9s_Caicedo) scored an equaliser, but shortly after, [Kalidou Koulibaly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalidou_Koulibaly) gave Senegal the victory. The win was enough to qualify Senegal as the runners-up of Group A.[^cite_note-133]

### Group B

Main article: [2022 FIFA World Cup Group B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_Group_B)

Group B began with England completing a 6–2 victory over Iran. Iranian keeper [Alireza Beiranvand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alireza_Beiranvand) was removed from the game for a suspected [concussion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concussion) before England scored three first-half goals.[^cite_note-EngIrn-134] [Mehdi Taremi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mehdi_Taremi) scored in the second half after which England defender [Harry Maguire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harry_Maguire) was also removed for a concussion.[^cite_note-EngIrn-134] [Timothy Weah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timothy_Weah), of the United States, scored a first-half goal against Wales; however, the match finished as a draw after a penalty kick was won and scored by [Gareth Bale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gareth_Bale).[^cite_note-135] Iran defeated Wales 2–0 following a red card to Welsh goalkeeper [Wayne Hennessey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayne_Hennessey) after he committed a foul outside of his [penalty area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penalty_area). Substitute [Rouzbeh Cheshmi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rouzbeh_Cheshmi) scored the first goal eight minutes into stoppage time, followed by [Ramin Rezaeian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramin_Rezaeian) scoring three minutes later.[^cite_note-136] England and the United States played to a 0–0 draw, with only four shots on target between them, and one shot from Christian Pulisic hit the crossbar.[^cite_note-137] England won the group following a 3–0 win over Wales with a goal by [Phil Foden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phil_Foden) and two by [Marcus Rashford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marcus_Rashford).[^cite_note-138] With the United States needing no less than a win to advance, [Christian Pulisic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Pulisic) scored the winning goal while colliding with the opposing goalkeeper as the United States defeated Iran 1–0 to qualify for the round of 16.[^cite_note-139]

### Group C

Main article: [2022 FIFA World Cup Group C](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_Group_C)

Argentina took an early lead against Saudi Arabia after [Lionel Messi](2150841.md) scored a penalty kick after ten minutes; however, second-half goals by [Saleh Al-Shehri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saleh_Al-Shehri) and [Salem Al-Dawsari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salem_Al-Dawsari) won the match 2–1 for Saudi Arabia,[^cite_note-140] a result the media considered one of the biggest upsets in the history of the event.[^cite_note-141][^cite_note-142] The match between Mexico and Poland ended as a goalless 0–0 draw after [Guillermo Ochoa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guillermo_Ochoa) saved [Robert Lewandowski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Lewandowski)'s penalty kick attempt.[^cite_note-143] Lewandowski scored his first career World Cup goal in a 2–0 win over Saudi Arabia four days later.[^cite_note-144][^cite_note-145] Argentina defeated Mexico 2–0, with Messi scoring the opener and later assisting teammate [Enzo Fernández](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enzo_Fern%C3%A1ndez) who scored his first international goal.[^cite_note-146][^cite_note-147] Argentina won their last game against Poland with goals from [Alexis Mac Allister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexis_Mac_Allister) and [Julián Álvarez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juli%C3%A1n_%C3%81lvarez), which was enough to win the group;[^cite_note-148] Poland qualified for the knockout stage on goal difference, thanks to Saudi Arabia scoring an injury-time consolation goal against Mexico in a match already lost.[^cite_note-149]

### Group D

Main article: [2022 FIFA World Cup Group D](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_Group_D)

The match between Denmark and Tunisia ended as a goalless draw; both teams had goals disallowed by offside calls.[^cite_note-SKYDenTun-150] Danish midfielder [Christian Eriksen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Eriksen) made his first major international appearance since suffering a cardiac arrest at the [UEFA Euro 2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2020).[^cite_note-SKYDenTun-150] Defending champions France went a goal behind to Australia, after a [Craig Goodwin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Craig_Goodwin) goal within ten minutes. France, however, scored four goals, by [Adrien Rabiot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adrien_Rabiot), [Kylian Mbappé](48711857.md) and two by [Olivier Giroud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olivier_Giroud) to win 4–1.[^cite_note-GuardianFraAus-151] The goals tied Giroud with [Thierry Henry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thierry_Henry) as France's all-time top goalscorer.[^cite_note-GuardianFraAus-151] [Mitchell Duke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mitchell_Duke) scored the only goal as Australia won against Tunisia. This was their first World Cup win since 2010.[^cite_note-auto4-152][^cite_note-153] Mbappé scored a brace as France defeated Denmark 2–1. This was enough for France to qualify for the knockout round—the first time since Brazil in 2006 that the defending champions progressed through the opening round.[^cite_note-auto4-152][^cite_note-154] [Mathew Leckie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mathew_Leckie) scored the only goal as Australia defeated Denmark 1–0, qualifying for the knockout round as runners-up with the win.[^cite_note-155] [Wahbi Khazri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wahbi_Khazri) scored for Tunisia against France in the 58th minute. Although [Antoine Griezmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antoine_Griezmann) equalised in stoppage time it was overturned for offside. Tunisia finished third in the group, as they required a draw in the Denmark and Australia game.[^cite_note-156]

### Group E

Main article: [2022 FIFA World Cup Group E](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_Group_E)

Group E began with Japan facing [2014 champions](656933.md) Germany. After an early penalty kick was converted by Germany's [İlkay Gündoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C4%B0lkay_G%C3%BCndo%C4%9Fan), Japan scored two second-half goals by [Ritsu Dōan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ritsu_D%C5%8Dan) and [Takuma Asano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Takuma_Asano) in a 2–1 win.[^cite_note-157] In the second group match, Spain defeated Costa Rica 7–0. First-half goals by [Dani Olmo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dani_Olmo), [Marco Asensio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marco_Asensio), and [Ferran Torres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferran_Torres) were followed by goals by [Gavi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gavi_\(footballer\)), [Carlos Soler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carlos_Soler_\(footballer\)), [Alvaro Morata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alvaro_Morata), and a second by Torres.[^cite_note-158][^cite_note-159] This was the largest defeat in a World Cup since Portugal's victory over North Korea in the [2010 event](168079.md) by the same scoreline.[^cite_note-160] Costa Rica defeated Japan 1–0, with [Keysher Fuller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keysher_Fuller) scoring with Costa Rica's first shot on target of the tournament.[^cite_note-161] Germany and Spain drew 1–1, with [Álvaro Morata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%81lvaro_Morata) scoring for Spain and [Niclas Füllkrug](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niclas_F%C3%BCllkrug) scoring for Germany.[^cite_note-162][^cite_note-163][^cite_note-164] Morata scored the opening goal for Spain against Japan as they controlled the first half of the match.[^cite_note-\:10-165] Japan equalised on [Ritsu Doan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ritsu_Doan) before a second goal by [Ao Tanaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ao_Tanaka) was heavily investigated by VAR for the ball potentially being out of play.[^cite_note-Bunshun.jp_interview_Josek-166] The goal was awarded, and Japan won the group following a 2–1 win. Disputes over the VAR's call was only settled when the [Associated Press](18935732.md) released an iconic [bird's eye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bird's_eye_view) photo of the ball after the match.[^cite_note-Bunshun.jp_interview_Josek-166] [Serge Gnabry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serge_Gnabry) scored on ten minutes for Germany against Costa Rica and they led until half-time. Germany required a win, and for Japan to not win their match, or for both teams to win their matches by a combined goal difference of at least 9 goals, to qualify. In the second half, goals by [Yeltsin Tejeda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yeltsin_Tejeda) and [Juan Vargas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Pablo_Vargas) gave Costa Rica a 2–1 lead, which would have qualified them into the knockout stages ahead of Spain despite the heavy earlier defeat. Germany scored three further goals—two by [Kai Havertz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kai_Havertz) and a goal by Niclas Füllkrug, ending in a 4–2 win for Germany—which was not enough to qualify them for the final stages. This was the second time in a row that the four-time champions failed to progress past the group stages.[^cite_note-167] Japan won the group ahead of Spain.[^cite_note-168]

### Group F

Main article: [2022 FIFA World Cup Group F](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_Group_F)

Group F's first match was a goalless draw between Morocco and Croatia.[^cite_note-169] Canada had a penalty kick in the first half of their match against Belgium which was saved by [Thibaut Courtois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thibaut_Courtois). Belgium won the match by a single goal by [Michy Batshuayi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michy_Batshuayi).[^cite_note-170][^cite_note-171] Belgium lost 2–0 to Morocco, despite Morocco having a long-range [direct free kick goal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_kick_\(association_football\)) by [Hakim Ziyech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hakim_Ziyech) overturned for an offside on another player in the lead up to the goal. Two second-half goals from [Zakaria Aboukhlal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zakaria_Aboukhlal) and [Romain Saïss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romain_Sa%C3%AFss) helped the Morocco win their first World Cup match since 1998.[^cite_note-172][^cite_note-173] The match sparked riots in Belgium, with fires and fireworks being set off by residents.[^cite_note-174] [Alphonso Davies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alphonso_Davies) scored Canada's first World Cup goal to give Canada the lead over Croatia. Goals by [Marko Livaja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marko_Livaja), [Lovro Majer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lovro_Majer), and two by [Andrej Kramarić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrej_Kramari%C4%87) for Croatia completed a 4–1 victory.[^cite_note-175] Morocco scored two early goals through [Hakim Ziyech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hakim_Ziyech) and [Youssef En-Nesyri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Youssef_En-Nesyri) in their game against Canada and qualified following a 2–1 victory. Canada's only goal was an [own goal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Own_goal) by [Nayef Aguerd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nayef_Aguerd).[^cite_note-176] Croatia and Belgium played a goalless draw which eliminated Belgium, whose team was ranked second in the world, from the tournament.[^cite_note-177]

### Group G

Main article: [2022 FIFA World Cup Group G](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_Group_G)

[Breel Embolo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Breel_Embolo) scored the only goal in Switzerland's 1–0 defeat of Cameroon.[^cite_note-178] [Richarlison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richarlison) scored two goals as Brazil won against Serbia, with star player [Neymar](15326144.md) receiving an ankle injury.[^cite_note-179] Cameroon's [Jean-Charles Castelletto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Charles_Castelletto) scored the opening goal against Serbia, but they were quickly behind as Serbia scored three goals by [Strahinja Pavlović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strahinja_Pavlovi%C4%87), [Sergej Milinković-Savić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergej_Milinkovi%C4%87-Savi%C4%87), and [Aleksandar Mitrović](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleksandar_Mitrovi%C4%87) either side of half time. Cameroon, however, scored goals through [Vincent Aboubakar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vincent_Aboubakar) and [Eric Maxim Choupo-Moting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eric_Maxim_Choupo-Moting), completing a 3–3 draw.[^cite_note-180] An 83rd-minute winner by [Casemiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casemiro) for Brazil over Switzerland was enough for them to qualify for the knockout stage.[^cite_note-181] Having already qualified, Brazil were unable to win their final group game, as they were defeated by Cameroon 1–0 following a goal by [Vincent Aboubakar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vincent_Aboubakar). Already having a [yellow card](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yellow_card_\(soccer\)), he was later sent off for removing his shirt in celebrating the goal.[^cite_note-182] Cameroon, however, did not qualify, as Switzerland defeated Serbia 3–2.[^cite_note-183]

### Group H

Main article: [2022 FIFA World Cup Group H](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_Group_H)

Uruguay and South Korea played to a goalless draw.[^cite_note-184] A goalless first half between Portugal and Ghana preceded a penalty converted by [Cristiano Ronaldo](623737.md) to give Portugal the lead. In scoring the goal, Ronaldo became the first man to score in five World Cups. Ghana responded with a goal by [André Ayew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andr%C3%A9_Ayew) before goals by [João Félix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jo%C3%A3o_F%C3%A9lix), and [Rafael Leão](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Le%C3%A3o) by Portugal put them 3–1 ahead. [Osman Bukari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osman_Bukari) scored in the 89th minute to trail by a single goal, while [Iñaki Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I%C3%B1aki_Williams) had a chance to equalise for Ghana ten minutes into stoppage time, but slipped before shooting. The match finished 3–2 to Portugal.[^cite_note-185] Ghanaian [Mohammed Salisu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_Salisu) opened the scoring against South Korea, with [Mohammed Kudus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_Kudus) following it up. In the second half, [Cho Gue-sung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cho_Gue-sung) scored a brace for South Korea, levelling the score. Mohammed Kudus scored again in the 68th minute, winning the match 3–2 for Ghana.[^cite_note-186] Portugal defeated Uruguay 2–0 with two goals from [Bruno Fernandes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruno_Fernandes), advancing them to the knockout stage.[^cite_note-187] A controversial penalty decision was called late in the game, with a suspected handball from [José María Giménez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Mar%C3%ADa_Gim%C3%A9nez).[^cite_note-188][^cite_note-189] Portugal led South Korea through [Ricardo Horta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ricardo_Horta) after 10 minutes. However, goals by [Kim Young-gwon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Young-gwon) and [Hwang Hee-chan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hwang_Hee-chan) won the match 2–1 for South Korea.[^cite_note-190] [Giorgian de Arrascaeta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giorgian_de_Arrascaeta) scored two goals as Uruguay defeated Ghana 2–0.[^cite_note-191] However, with South Korea winning, Uruguay required another goal to progress as they finished third on goals scored.[^cite_note-192] Several Uruguay players left the pitch after the game surrounding the referees and followed them off the pitch.[^cite_note-193][^cite_note-194][^cite_note-195]

## Knockout stage

Main article: [2022 FIFA World Cup knockout stage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_knockout_stage)

In the knockout stage, if the scores were equal when normal playing time expired, [extra time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overtime_\(Association_football\)) was played for two periods of 15 minutes each. This was followed, if required, by a [penalty shoot-out](511302.md) to determine the winners.[^cite_note-regulations-121]

All times are local, [AST](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabia_Standard_Time) ([UTC+3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UTC+03\:00)).

### Round of 16

The round of 16 was played from 3 to 7 December,[^cite_note-schedule-22] which for the first time ever included teams from each of the six inhabited continents.[^cite_note-196] Group A winners Netherlands scored goals through [Memphis Depay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memphis_Depay), [Daley Blind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daley_Blind), and [Denzel Dumfries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denzel_Dumfries) as they defeated the United States 3–1, with [Haji Wright](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haji_Wright) scoring for the United States.[^cite_note-197] Messi scored his third of the tournament alongside [Julián Álvarez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juli%C3%A1n_%C3%81lvarez_\(footballer\)) to give Argentina a two-goal lead over Australia, and despite an Enzo Fernández own goal from a [Craig Goodwin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Craig_Goodwin) shot, Argentina won 2–1.[^cite_note-198] [Olivier Giroud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olivier_Giroud)'s goal and Mbappé's brace enabled France to have a 3–1 victory over Poland, with [Robert Lewandowski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Lewandowski) scoring the lone goal for Poland from a penalty.[^cite_note-199] England beat Senegal 3–0, with goals coming from [Jordan Henderson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_Henderson), [Harry Kane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harry_Kane), and [Bukayo Saka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bukayo_Saka).[^cite_note-200] [Daizen Maeda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daizen_Maeda) scored for Japan against Croatia in the first half before a leveller from [Ivan Perišić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivan_Peri%C5%A1i%C4%87) in the second. Neither team could find the winner, with Croatia defeating Japan 3–1 in a [penalty shoot-out](511302.md).[^cite_note-201] [Vinícius Júnior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vin%C3%ADcius_J%C3%BAnior), [Neymar](15326144.md), [Richarlison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richarlison), and [Lucas Paquetá](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucas_Paquet%C3%A1) all scored for Brazil, but a volley from South Korean [Paik Seung-ho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paik_Seung-ho) reduced the deficit to 4–1.[^cite_note-202] [Pablo Sarabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pablo_Sarabia) had the best chance for Spain to break the deadlock against Morocco in stoppage time after two goalless hours, but he hit the goalie's right-hand post after a shot from the left; Morocco won the match 3–0 on penalties.[^cite_note-203] A [hat-trick](411541.md) by [Gonçalo Ramos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gon%C3%A7alo_Ramos) led Portugal to defeat Switzerland 6–1, with goals from Portugal's [Pepe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pepe_\(footballer,_born_1983\)), [Raphaël Guerreiro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rapha%C3%ABl_Guerreiro), and [Rafael Leão](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafael_Le%C3%A3o) and from Switzerland's [Manuel Akanji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_Akanji).[^cite_note-204]

### Quarter-finals

The quarter-finals were played on 9 and 10 December.[^cite_note-schedule-22] Croatia and Brazil ended 0–0 after 90 minutes and went to extra time. Neymar scored for Brazil in the 15th minute of extra time. Croatia, however, equalised through [Bruno Petković](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruno_Petkovi%C4%87) in the second period of extra time. With the match tied, a penalty shoot-out decided the contest, with Croatia winning the shoot-out 4–2.[^cite_note-213][^cite_note-214] The second quarter-final match, between Argentina and the Netherlands, later dubbed as the [Battle of Lusail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Lusail) saw [Nahuel Molina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nahuel_Molina) and Messi score for Argentina before [Wout Weghorst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wout_Weghorst) equalised with two goals shortly before the end of the game. The match went to extra time and then penalties, where Argentina would go on to win 4–3.[^cite_note-215] Morocco defeated Portugal 1–0, with [Youssef En-Nesyri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Youssef_En-Nesyri) scoring at the end of the first half. Morocco became the first African and the first Arab nation to advance as far as the semi-finals of the competition.[^cite_note-216] Despite [Harry Kane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harry_Kane) scoring a penalty for England, it was not enough to beat France, who won 2–1 by virtue of goals from [Aurélien Tchouaméni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aur%C3%A9lien_Tchouam%C3%A9ni) and [Olivier Giroud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olivier_Giroud) and a late missed penalty by Kane, sending them to their second consecutive World Cup semi-final and becoming the first defending champions to reach this stage since Brazil in 1998.[^cite_note-217][^cite_note-218]

### Semi-finals

The semi-finals were played on 13 and 14 December.[^cite_note-schedule-22] Messi scored a penalty kick before [Julián Álvarez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juli%C3%A1n_%C3%81lvarez_\(footballer\)) scored twice to give Argentina a 3–0 victory over Croatia.[^cite_note-223] [Théo Hernandez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Th%C3%A9o_Hernandez) scored after five minutes as France led Morocco for most of the game. [Randal Kolo Muani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Randal_Kolo_Muani) scored in the 78th minute to complete a 2–0 victory for France over Morocco as they reached their second consecutive final.[^cite_note-224]

### Match for third place

The match for third place was played on 17 December. The two teams had played against each other in their [opening game in Group F](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_Group_F#Morocco_vs_Croatia) which finished 0–0. [Joško Gvardiol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jo%C5%A1ko_Gvardiol) promptly scored for Croatia, with [Achraf Dari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Achraf_Dari) equalising just 2 minutes later. [Mislav Oršić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mislav_Or%C5%A1i%C4%87) scored the winner for Croatia as the match finished 2–1.[^cite_note-227] Morocco earned 4th place, a record for the team and the best World Cup finish of any African or Arab nation.[^cite_note-228]

### Final

Main article: [2022 FIFA World Cup final](70432805.md)

The final was played on 18 December between Argentina and France. Both teams had won the event twice previously.[^cite_note-final1-230] Early goals from Lionel Messi and [Ángel Di María](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%81ngel_Di_Mar%C3%ADa) gave Argentina a 2–0 lead against the French.[^cite_note-final1-230][^cite_note-final2-231] Despite substitutions in the first half, France did not record a shot until after the 70th minute but were energised by additional substitutions in the 71st. A few minutes later, France were awarded a penalty as [Randal Kolo Muani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Randal_Kolo_Muani) was brought down in the penalty area by [Nicolás Otamendi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicol%C3%A1s_Otamendi). Mbappé scored the penalty and added a second goal less than two minutes later to equalise the scores.[^cite_note-final2-231] With the score tied at two goals apiece, the match went to extra time. Messi scored his second goal in the 108th minute, once again giving Argentina the lead. However, Mbappé was awarded a second penalty in the 115th minute after his shot hit the arm of [Gonzalo Montiel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gonzalo_Montiel). Mbappé scored his third goal, becoming the second player ever to complete a [hat-trick](411541.md) in the final of a World Cup after [Geoff Hurst](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geoff_Hurst) for England in [1966](61629.md).[^cite_note-final2-231] With the score tied at 3–3, the match was determined via a penalty shoot-out. Argentina won the final after scoring all of their penalties, winning 4–2.[^cite_note-final2-231] This marked their third World Cup win and their first since [1986](157231.md), and also the second team (after [Spain](609825.md) in [2010](168079.md)) to win the tournament after having lost their opening game.[^cite_note-final2-231]

## Statistics

See also: [FIFA World Cup records and statistics](3602475.md)

### Goalscorers

Note: Goals scored in penalty shoot-outs are not counted towards players' goal counts or to match goal counts.



France forward [Kylian Mbappé](48711857.md) won the [Golden Boot award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_Golden_Boot) after scoring eight goals in the tournament, the most since [Ronaldo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronaldo_\(Brazilian_footballer\)) in [2002](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_FIFA_World_Cup).

There were 172 goals scored in 64 matches, for an average of 2.69 goals per match.

Two own goals were scored throughout the tournament.[^cite_note-233]

This was the most goals ever scored at a FIFA World Cup.[^cite_note-234]

### Discipline

A player was automatically suspended for the next match for the following offences:[^cite_note-regulations-121]

- Receiving a red card (red card suspensions could be extended for serious offences)

- Receiving two yellow cards in two matches; yellow cards expired after the completion of the quarter-finals (yellow card suspensions were not carried forward to any other future international matches)

The following suspensions were served during the tournament:

## Awards



Argentina captain and forward [Lionel Messi](2150841.md) won the [Golden Ball award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_Golden_Ball), becoming the first player to win it twice.

The following [World Cup awards](3482503.md) were given at the conclusion of the tournament: the [Golden Boot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_Golden_Boot) (top scorer), [Golden Ball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_Golden_Ball) (best overall player) and [Golden Glove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_Golden_Glove) (best goalkeeper).[^cite_note-243]

Additionally, FIFA.com shortlisted 10 goals for users to vote on as the tournament's best. The award was won by Brazil's [Richarlison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richarlison) for his second goal in the [group stage match against Serbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_Group_G#Brazil_vs_Serbia).[^cite_note-244]

## Marketing

### Branding

The official emblem was designed by Unlock Brands[^cite_note-245] and unveiled in September 2019, during simultaneous events at the [Doha Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doha_Tower), [Katara Cultural Village](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katara_Cultural_Village) amphitheatre, [Msheireb Downtown Doha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Msheireb_Downtown_Doha), and [Zubarah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zubarah). It is based on a [shawl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shawl) (reflecting upon the tournament's late-Autumn scheduling) and desert [dunes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dune), and was shaped to resemble the [tournament trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_Trophy), the [infinity symbol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infinity_symbol), and the number "8"—reflecting upon the "interconnected" event and the eight host stadiums. The typography of the emblem's [wordmark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wordmark) incorporated [kashida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kashida)—the practice of elongating certain parts of characters in [Arabic script](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_script) to provide typographical emphasis.[^cite_note-246][^cite_note-247][^cite_note-248]

H.E Ambassador Dr. Hend Al-Muftah, Permanent Representative of the State of Qatar to the United Nations Office at Geneva, explained to [WIPO Magazine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WIPO_Magazine) that the tournament provided an opportunity to "promote our culture and showcase our signature hospitality in a multicultural environment", and highlighted that the country has [used sports to](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_and_sports) demonstrate its "commitment to sustainable development, social inclusion and the values of community and mutual respect among peoples from different cultures and nations."[^cite_note-249]

### Merchandise

See also: [FIFA World Cup video games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup_video_games)

[Electronic Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronic_Arts)' licensed video games *[FIFA Mobile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_Mobile)* and *[FIFA 23](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIFA_23)* (except on [Nintendo Switch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nintendo_Switch)) received free updates with tie-in content for the 2022 World Cup on 8 and 9 November 2022, respectively. The update for *FIFA 23* added World Cup-themed tournament modes with all teams and two of the stadiums from the event, campaigns and World Cup-themed unlockable content for Ultimate Team mode, and real-time squad and fixture updates during the tournament.[^cite_note-250]

On 24 August 2022, the [Panini Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panini_Group) produced themed stickers and a [sticker album](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sticker_album) for a 14th consecutive World Cup. This year, rare cards with coloured borders were included and could be collected, [traded](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trading_cards), or sold.[^cite_note-251]

On 12 April 2022, [FIFA](11049.md) released an [over-the-top media service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Over-the-top_media_service) and app revolving around the World Cup called FIFA+, where fans could play games, predict matches, and compete with others.[^cite_note-252]

### Broadcasting rights

Main article: [2022 FIFA World Cup broadcasting rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_FIFA_World_Cup_broadcasting_rights)

In May 2022, Infantino projected that the 2022 FIFA World Cup could be the most-watched in its history, with a global audience of at least 5 billion. The 2018 tournament was seen by 3.57 billion across the tournament.[^cite_note-253] The various controversies surrounding the World Cup in Qatar led to questions over how the tournament would be covered in the media, and whether they would be discussed or addressed during coverage.[^cite_note-254][^cite_note-\:1-255] The match between England and the United States in the group stage was seen by approximately 20 million viewers across both Fox and [Telemundo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telemundo), ranking among the highest-rated associated football broadcasts in the United States (behind a 2014 World Cup match that drew 24.7 million).[^cite_note-256][^cite_note-257]

### Sponsorship



[Qatar Airways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qatar_Airways) was one of the [sponsors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sponsor_\(commercial\)) of FIFA World Cup 2022.

The sponsors of the 2022 World Cup are divided into seven categories: FIFA Partners, FIFA World Cup Sponsors and African and Middle Eastern, Asian, European, North American and South American supporters. This marks the first time a FIFA World Cup is sponsored by supporters from seven different regions.
